## Sheet: Overview
| PEMS - Platform Overview |
| --- |
| Read this sheet first, before Read Me / Instructions / Backlog, in any new session. It is the fastest orientation to what PEMS is, where it is going and how work is done here. Update it whenever something it describes goes stale (not only Instructions/Memory). |
| What PEMS is |
| PEMS is an enterprise .NET system (ASP.NET Core MVC/Razor + EF Core 10 + SQL Server) for engineering and maintenance management in the oil and petrochemical industry: EDMS/VDR, CMMS/EAM, permit to work (PTW), inventory, production, reliability and local AI (Ollama/OCR). Today it is a set of fixed modules (98 controllers, 91 View folders, 116 migrations, 1,403 tests). PEMS is a brand-new product whose long-term aim is to become the single platform for its customers' engineering and maintenance processes. |
| New direction: from fixed software to a dynamic BPMS |
| Forms, modules and workflows must be fully creatable and editable by authorized users, with no coding and no build/deploy. Today's fixed modules are progressively converted into metadata definitions (strangler pattern, backlog area MIG) and specialized parts (AI, OCR, EDMS revision engine, reliability) stay as Domain Packs with extension points. Shared engines: metadata registry, form, workflow, rules, access/RLS, events, reporting. |
| Commercial model |
| For now, an on-prem licence locked to the machine and the install time: even if a whole machine is imaged and deployed elsewhere it must not work. Process: a request file from the customer server, returned to the vendor, a signed licence file issued, installed. Licence types: lifetime, term (a time period), trial, demo. Details in the Licensing Design sheet and the LIC area. The product never calls out (production has no internet); the website and the demo are the only online parts. |
| Prioritization rule of this backlog |
| Priority is set by 'ability to sell' and 'dynamic core': P0 = without it nothing can be sold or the dynamic platform cannot form (foundation decisions, metadata, grid/form, licensing, RLS, demo, install); P1 = needed for commercial release 1.0; P2 = competitive/next stage; P3 = later. The legacy domain backlog (CMMS/EDMS etc.) matters but stays behind sales and dynamicization work unless it blocks them. |
| Current state (estimated from code, no build executed) |
| Strengths: domain breadth, modern authentication (Identity + FIDO2), a central access model, a workflow runtime, local AI. Main gaps: no App/Entity/Form registry and no form builder; no workflow studio; no licensing and no installer; no server-side session registry; no Jalali UI; default culture is en. No build/test has been run (row F_BASE). Database creation, install and update are fully code-first (migrator + Admin Database Update page, 2026-10-22). |
| Development strategy |
| Plan-file-driven: this workbook is the single source of truth for task status and is updated within each task. First scope, then update the plan, then design with a library-first, best-practice approach, then implement. Go forward, not backward-compatible; applied migrations are never edited. Marketing claims cover only what is Done and tested in the Backlog. |
| Where to go next |
| 'Is X done / status of Y?' -> Backlog (Evidence column). 'What is next?' -> Roadmap. 'Which rules always apply?' -> Instructions. 'What do we know from earlier platforms?' -> Lessons. 'Which capabilities must PEMS offer?' -> Capability Checklist. 'How does licensing work?' -> Licensing Design. 'What is supported and up to which limits?' -> Supportability Matrix. 'What happened to an old task?' -> Old to New Map. |
## Sheet: Read Me
| PEMS - Backlog & Roadmap (rebuilt edition) |  |
| --- | --- |
| Created 2026-10-15 from the static PEMS audit plus lessons and capabilities of a reference platform. The filename is deliberately date-free and the file is updated in place. |  |
| Sheets |  |
| Sheet | Purpose |
| Overview | Orientation - read first. |
| Read Me | This sheet: column guide, statuses, priorities. |
| Instructions | Standing rules applied in every task. |
| Backlog | The unified task list (source of truth). |
| Test Plan | QA cases for Done rows and for P0 rows after implementation. |
| Roadmap | Milestones and execution order. |
| Supportability Matrix | What is supported (capabilities, limits, platforms, versions) - updated in every finished task. |
| Licensing Design | Machine/install-locked licensing design and threat table. |
| Capability Checklist | Capabilities PEMS must offer, sourced from the reference platform. |
| Lessons | Durable lessons and facts (reference platform and PEMS). |
| Old to New Map | Mapping of every old backlog row to the new rows. |
| Summary | Automatic counts (formulas) over the Backlog. |
| Backlog columns |  |
| Column | Meaning |
| # | Permanent number; never reused. |
| Doer | Claude = doable in a code session; User = decision or legal/commercial/operational work of the owner. |
| Area | Work area. |
| Track | Feature, Bug Fix, Tech Debt, Decision Needed, Library Modernization, Research, Docs, Legal/Business, Ops Process. |
| Status | Not Started, In Progress, Partial, Done, Hold (decided but paused by the PM until released), Later (deliberately deferred, kept in the backlog), Cancelled, Obsolete. Done only with evidence. |
| Confirmed by PM | Yes when the owner/PM has reviewed and confirmed the row (its scope, priority and milestone); No until then. Only the PM sets this flag; automated updates of this file preserve what the PM entered. |
| Sprint | Sprint the row is planned in (Sprint 1, Sprint 1 stretch); empty = backlog. The Sprint 1 sheet has goal, capacity and the committed list. |
| Owner | Accountable person for the row in a sprint (Member A / Member B until names are mapped); the doer may still be the assistant. |
| Group | Related tasks grouped into one workstream; filter on this column to see and decide a whole group together. |
| Priority | P0 sales blocker/dynamic core, P1 needed for commercial 1.0, P2 competitive, P3 later. |
| Evidence | What was seen in code/documents today (or a known pitfall). |
| Prompt | An executable instruction for one session. |
| Requirement | A testable acceptance criterion. |
| Report | After completion: the commit message and version (Instructions rule 7). |
| Milestone | M0...M6 per Roadmap. |
| Effort | S up to 1 day, M about 1 week, L about 2-3 weeks, XL over 3 weeks (first estimate). |
| Commercial | Commercial impact: Sales blocker, High, Medium, Low. |
| Depends | Dependencies by row number (#). |
| Reference (Titan) | Related row number in the reference platform's backlog (Titan); shown as # numbers. |
| Old Ref | Related row in the old audit (OLD-n) or OLD-Dn for existing capabilities. |

## Sheet: Instructions
| Standing instructions for the PEMS project |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Apply these in every task even when the request does not restate them. Keep in sync with Memory; a rule changes only with the owner's decision. |  |  |
| # | Rule | Description |
| 1 | Offline execution | Production has no internet: no CDN, external call, telemetry or online licence/update check is allowed in the product. Any new library must run fully offline and not depend on an online source at build time. (The website and demo are online parts and are separate from the product.) |
| 2 | Library-first, with licence control | Before hand-building (expression engine, grid, workflow designer, rules, scheduler, PDF/Excel) check for a mature library. For commercial sale every library licence (copyleft, revenue thresholds, mandatory watermark) must be recorded in row L_OSS; an unsuitable library is replaced. Custom code only with a stated reason. |
| 3 | Engines once, not per module | Each shared capability (metadata, forms, workflow, access/RLS, events, reporting, licensing) has exactly one implementation; modules call it. Parallel concepts are forbidden. |
| 4 | No new static modules | From now on a new module is built first as metadata (form/view/workflow). If that is impossible, record the reason and the needed extension point in an ADR. A new static module without this record is not allowed. |
| 5 | Persian-first, English file | The product default language is Persian with RTL; displayed dates are Jalali with Gregorian/UTC storage. This workbook, code identifiers and ADRs are written in English. |
| 6 | Go forward, not backward-compatible | During development applied migrations are not edited; always add a new numbered migration/seed. No backward-compatibility shim unless a live customer needs it. |
| 7 | Task workflow | Before starting set the row to In Progress; afterwards commit separately with a version bump and CHANGELOG, then update Status/Report in this file as its own commit. Message format 'vX.Y.Z: description'. Never use 'git add -A'; stage only specific files. |
| 8 | Order of work: scope, plan, design, implement | (a) Everything the user said, including later clarifications; (b) update the backlog row before designing; (c) then design library-first with best practice; (d) then implement. |
| 9 | Three version numbers | System = CalVer (YYYY.MM.PATCH); components = SemVer; editing this file does not change a version. The component table is on the Read Me sheet. |
| 10 | Secrets and keys | No real secret, key or connection string in the repo or release package. The licence-signing private key and the reset private key are created and used only on the owner computers (Hamid's and Saber's own) and are never in the repo, on a CI or shared server, or in the assistant's environment (L_KEYS). |
| 11 | Licence active everywhere | Licence checks apply in all environments (a signed dev licence); no bypass flag may reach any package. Always-free surfaces come only from an allowlist (L_FREE). |
| 12 | Central RLS | No data path (list, search, report, export, API, AI) is without the central RLS filter; direct role-based checks are not allowed. |
| 13 | Claims only from Done | Sales material, website and demo claim only Done capabilities with acceptance tests. 'Done' without evidence (file:line, commit or test) is not allowed. |
| 14 | QA of Done rows | A Done row whose Test Plan status is 'Needs QA' is not yet trustworthy; test it before using it in sales. |
| 15 | Fix anti-patterns in scope | If work passes through clearly poor code, fix it within that scope; large or risky fixes get their own row. |
| 16 | Finish, then stop | Complete the current task with all its closing steps, then propose a short list of next tasks and wait for the owner's choice. |
| 17 | No customer-specific forks | Customer differences live only in the licence (edition, modules, limits, updates-until), configuration, customer metadata packages and extension packs. There are no per-customer branches or builds; every shipped package comes from a tagged release and is recorded in the customer registry (CV_REG). |
| 18 | Landing and demo are separate deployables | The landing website (main domain) and the demo (subdomain) share one host but nothing else: separate source (the landing website in its own repository pems-landing, the demo in the demo branch of the product repository), pipelines, deploy accounts, secrets, environments, monitoring and rollback. A deployment, reset or failure of one must never touch the other. Both are deployed by GitHub Actions; if the host is unreachable the pipeline publishes the artifact for a manual deployment that follows the same script. |
| 19 | PM confirmation | A row is Confirmed by PM only when the owner says so. Claude never sets the flag on its own initiative except to record a decision the owner has just given, and never clears it. Rows on Hold stay scheduled but are not started until the PM releases them. |
| 20 | UI-only operations | Licensing, installation, update and version-reporting operations, for the customer and for the vendor, must be completable in a UI (browser pages or the graphical installer and vendor tool). A command line may exist only as an optional extra for automation, never as the only way. |
| 21 | Supportability Matrix in every task | The Supportability Matrix sheet is updated in every finished task: add or change the rows for what the task makes supported (capability or limit, component, supported value, version, verifying backlog row). Builder limits and other supported values come only from this matrix. Record the matrix update in the task Report. |
| 22 | Every task touches six things | Memory (if something was learned), the plan file, the Supportability Matrix, Instructions (only if a rule changes), CHANGELOG and a commit. |
| 23 | Schema only through migrations | Owner decision 2026-10-22: the database is fully code-first. A new environment is installed and every release updated by the migrator (startup or Admin > Database Update); no hand-run SQL for schema setup or updates. Needed DDL that EF cannot model goes into a migration (migrationBuilder.Sql); superseded SQL moves to legacy-trash/, it is not deleted until the owner agrees. |
| 24 | Settings in env files, secrets apart, nothing hard-coded | Owner decision 2026-10-23: every setting lives in env/<name>.env, every secret only in env/<name>.secrets.env (git-ignored) or an environment variable, for local, docker, demo, uat and production-<customer>. No environment-dependent value (URL, host, credential, port, lifetime, culture, policy flag) is hard-coded in code; a new setting is added to every env file and read with Required*. Customer differences are the production-<customer>.env file and the licence (rule 17). |
| 25 | Plan, the issue tracker and code stay consistent | Owner decision 2026-10-23 (docs/process/PLAN_TRACKER_WORKFLOW.md): the Backlog # is the Plan ID and is stored in the tracker in a Plan ID field; the workbook itself contains no tracker-specific fields. The workbook owns scope (title, priority, milestone, requirement, dependencies), the tracker owns status, assignee and sprint. Branches, commits and PR titles start with the issue key. The workbook changes only by pull request and plan/backlog.csv is regenerated; plan-sync check must pass. |
| 26 | Components only for new UI | Owner decision 2026-10-23 (docs/design/COMPONENT_LIBRARY_PLAN.md): once PEMS.UI exists, new pages are built from its components and templates; raw form-control/table/modal/badge markup, inline styles, inline scripts and CDN references are not added (CI ratchet). A missing component is added to the library first. |
| 27 | Keep the Jira report current | Owner decision 2026-10-26: every task keeps its Jira issue report up to date while the work runs and when it ends, not only at the end. (1) At start: comment 'Started by <agent or person>' with the plan. (2) During the work: a short comment at every milestone, decision, blocker or change of scope (what is done, what is next, what blocks), and a comment before any hand-over or stop. (3) At the end: a closing comment with what changed, files, tests run and not run, PR link, evidence for each 'Done when' item and open items for the owner. The same text goes in the PR description. An issue without a current report is not moved to In Review or Done. Never put a secret value in a report (mask to two characters at most). |
| 28 | Study existing tasks before adding one | Owner decision 2026-10-26: before adding a task (backlog row and Jira issue) read the existing ones - search the backlog and Jira by area, files, goal and keywords, including Done and Hold rows. If an existing task covers the work or can absorb it, modify that task (extend What to do / Done when, update the workbook row, the Jira issue and a comment saying what changed and why) instead of adding a new one. Add a new row only when no task fits, and name the tasks that were checked in its Evidence. A new row keeps the next free Plan ID; held (Hold) tasks count as not open. |

| 309 | Claude | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Fully code-first database: automatic install on first run and automatic update on later runs (EF Core migrations, full-text search as a migration); no InitialCreate.sql, no SSMS script, no manual 'ef database update' | Tech Debt | Partial | P0 | Was: EF Core code-first model + migration history, but nothing applied them at startup (Program.cs only ran seeders) and the full-text script was a manual SSMS step with a hardcoded 'USE PEMSDB'. Now: DatabaseInitializer (create database if missing before Hangfire connects) + IDatabaseUpdateService (see #310) run before the seeders; settings Database:CreateIfMissing / MigrateOnStartup; full-text search is the migration FullTextSearch; the manual schema SQL is moved to legacy-trash/ (#311); CI 'Model drift gate'; docs/deployment/CODE_FIRST_DATABASE.md. VERIFIED 2026-10-22 on SQL Server LocalDB: the real app started against a database that did not exist, created it, installed 116 migrations (Startup/FirstRun recorded), seeders ran with 0 errors, /health/database = 200; integration tests prove install -> up to date -> update and that an unmanaged schema is untouched. NOT yet verified: the CI drift gate on GitHub, a restart no-op in the full app, hosting on the real demo server (Plesk/IIS, SQL Server Full-Text feature, startup time limit). TESTS 2026-10-23 on LocalDB: Authorization 73/73, Search 16/16, Setup 12/12, MyWork 42/42, Web 232/235 (2 were missing localization keys for the new page - fixed; 1 needs SQLEXPRESS by design), Deployment 19/21 (2 failures in deploy/ artifact tests that this work does not touch: LF-only assertion on a Windows autocrlf checkout and the bash mock harness), new code-first tests 57/57 after fixes, Admin page driven in a browser (install shown, update available, backup confirmation enforced, update applied and recorded). The full 1,400-test suite was not completed on this machine (hours; LocalDB wedged by killed runs). | Push the branch, let build.yml run (drift gate), merge to demo and watch #312 install the demo database. | 315 | Claude | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Environment settings and secrets files for local, docker, demo, uat and production-kangan; no hard-coded settings in code | Tech Debt | Done | P0 | Owner request 2026-10-23. Before: settings in appsettings.json/.Development/.UAT plus literals in code (Fido2, Ollama URL, password policy, cookie, log retention, culture, built-in admin account and password, design-time connection string, docker-compose defaults). Now: env/<name>.env (settings, committed) and env/<name>.secrets.env (secrets, git-ignored, templates *.secrets.env.example); PEMS_ENV selects the file; loader PEMS.Shared EnvFiles + AddPemsEnvFiles (precedence: env file < secrets file < environment variables < command line); required settings fail fast; appsettings.json is empty; the first administrator comes from Seed:Admin:*; the dotnet ef factory reads the env files; docker-compose, Dockerfile, Build-Package and the demo pipeline use them. VERIFIED 2026-10-23: the real app booted on LocalDB from env/local.env + env/local.secrets.env alone (no environment variables), created and installed the database and seeded the admin, whose secret password signs in (a wrong one is refused); a missing env file and a missing required setting stop startup with a clear message; the web.config transformation yields well-formed XML. TESTS 2026-10-23: new env tests 10/10; Help 110/110, Letters 261/261, HTTP security host 9/9 (boots Program from the env files), Deployment 29/30 and Web 240/241 and Authorization 19/20 - the three remaining failures are pre-existing and unrelated (LF-only assertion on a Windows autocrlf checkout; a test that refuses non-SQLEXPRESS; ImportCenterNavigationTests flags LetterCompose and fails identically on a clean origin/main). | Run the demo pipeline once with the new secrets (#317); install on the Linux VM with PEMS_ENV set (#316). | Every setting is in an env file, every secret only in a secrets file or an environment variable, the committed files contain no secrets (test), all environments define the same keys (test), and the app starts from the files alone. | Local commits on branch temp/code-first-database ('feat(config): environment settings and secrets files ...'). | 343 | Claude | Access, RLS, Sessions & Security | Design and decision for the vendor-assisted admin password reset by file exchange (request file out, signed response file back), separate reset key, and AFTA compatibility check | Decision Needed | Not Started | P1 | A customer whose production server is offline must be able to recover a lost administrator password with the vendor's help, without a back door in the product and without any remote access. The licence flow already proves that file exchange works offline; the reset reuses its shape with its own key. Gate: this row can start only after #342 (licence flow end to end) is Done. Independent of the separate administrator-recovery work done by Saber (second-admin reset, one-time recovery codes, server-side break-glass, shared audit/notification/session revocation): that work does not wait for the licence flow and this row does not block it; this flow plugs into it as the optional vendor-assisted recovery path, which must stay off until the customer enables it. 2026-10-28 plan audit: dependency/milestone corrected (see docs/audits/2026-10-28-dynamic-restful-audit.md). | Write docs/design/ADMIN_RESET.md (no secrets). Flow (same shape as licence activation, all in the browser UI; no terminal or CLI step): 1) the customer opens the administrator recovery page on their installation and downloads a reset request file (.pemsrstreq: Install ID, fingerprint hash, licence id, product version, account, random nonce kept server-side as a pending request, signed by the install key); 2) the customer sends the file to us; 3) in the developer/owner UI we upload it, the tool verifies the signature, matches it to the customer and licence in the ledger, we confirm the customer, enter the reason and the identity check, and download a reset response file (.pemsreset) signed with the separate reset key and bound to the request nonce, licence id, Install ID, purpose and expiry; 4) the customer uploads the response file on the same page and the password reset form opens; the pending request is consumed so the file works once. Decide: reset-key custody. Decided 2026-10-25 by the owners: the licence key and the reset key are each created and used only on the owner computers (the own computers of Hamid and Saber, who are the owners and the developers; also called developer-local or owner machines), as the last step of the licence and reset flows. Neither key is ever in the assistant's environment, on a shared or CI server, or in the repository. The reset key is still a different key from the licence key. To settle in the ADR: whether the keys stay offline (encrypted external drive) between uses, where the backup copies are and who holds them, what the reset changes (password; MFA/passkeys and sessions as in the shared recovery rules), expiry of the response file, rate limits, audit on both sides, behaviour on a cloned or re-hosted machine (#104, #116). AFTA check (to be confirmed with the testing lab through #184): no default, master or hard-coded password and no standing vendor credential; the vendor can act only on a request that the installation itself generated, for one installation, once and for a short time; the capability is off by default and enabled explicitly by the customer administrator; every use is audited, all administrators are notified, the account sessions are revoked and the event goes to the SIEM feed; the reset key is separate from the licence key and can be revoked through an update; everything works offline by file exchange, so no remote access to the customer server is ever needed. Validity: the response file expires after a period the owner chooses in the developer UI when the file is issued: default 1 day, allowed range 1 to 7 days in whole days, and no unlimited or open-ended option (the tool refuses values outside the range and the product checks the limits again, so a correctly signed file with a longer validity is rejected). The pending reset request on the customer side expires on the same bounds. AFTA: a short, bounded, single-use validity fits the check recorded here; the 1-day default and the 7-day maximum are to be confirmed with the testing lab (#184), and if the lab wants a shorter maximum the limits change in one setting without changing the file format. | Done when: an approved ADR with the file formats' fields, the key-custody details on the owner computers, the threat model (replay, wrong customer, tampered or forged file, cloned machine, clock change, leaked file) and a written AFTA compatibility assessment accepted by the security consultant; #344-#349 are written against it. The validity rule (default 1 day, range 1 to 7 days, no unlimited option) is confirmed by the AFTA assessment. |  | M3 | M | Sales blocker | #96 #107 #178 #182 #339 #342 |  |  | Security & access | No |  |  |

| 355 | Claude | Metadata Core & Dynamic Platform | Field validation catalog: for every field type the constraints (required, range, length, decimals, size, count, pattern, date window, membership, uniqueness), their defaults, hard versus advisory, and the stored validation object | Research | Not Started | P0 | Validation in PEMS today is thin and scattered: 260 entities but only 26 [Required] and 45 [MaxLength] attributes in 28 files, no [Range], [StringLength] or regular-expression rule anywhere, no validation library, ModelState.IsValid is checked in 8 controllers, and 64 of 65 plain text inputs in the views have no maxlength. Dynamic forms need one declarative description of the rules per field type before any engine or UI is built. Rule-builder row #46 covers user-defined rules; this row defines the built-in constraints every type carries. 2026-10-28 plan audit: dependency/milestone corrected (see docs/audits/2026-10-28-dynamic-restful-audit.md). | 1) For every type in #11 list its constraints. Number/decimal/money: min, max, strict range, step, integer only, allow negative, allow zero, scale and precision. Date/date-time: min/max date, relative window (not in the past, not in the future, within N days), Jalali-aware, time zone. Boolean: must be true. Select/multi-select: membership, min/max selected. Lookup/user: existence, active only, scope. File/image: max size per file and in total, count, extensions, MIME, dimensions. Rich text: max length of the text after sanitisation. JSON: schema. Existing rows #24, #205, #206, #208 and #209 ask for translated labels, Persian digits, the Jalali calendar and fixed reports, but nothing defines where a field's format lives or how it relates to validation. A comparable implementation shows the risks: one hard-coded locale in its date component, date strings built without zero padding, an invalid-date defect in downloaded reports and a deprecated date library for the Jalali picker. 2026-10-28 plan audit: dependency/milestone corrected (see docs/audits/2026-10-28-dynamic-restful-audit.md). | 1) For each type in #11 define three things: the canonical value that is stored and exchanged (ISO 8601 date-only for dates, UTC instant for date-times, decimal with fixed scale for numbers and money, normalised text), the display format, and the input and parse format. 2) Presets with examples (date: Jalali long/short, Gregorian ISO; number: grouping, decimal separator, digit shapes Latin or Persian; currency: rial, toman, symbol position, negative style; percent; units; boolean: yes/no, tick; empty value: dash). 3) Where a format lives: a settings hierarchy system -> app -> model -> field -> user preference with a defined fallback (a model or field setting wins for content, the user's setting for chrome), stored in the metadata registry as a versioned format object next to the validation object. 4) Label-related formats: label, help, placeholder, error and option text as translatable strings (#24), unit and prefix/suffix, direction. 5) State the pipeline order and the rule that validation always runs on the canonical value. 6) Write docs/architecture/FIELD_FORMAT_CATALOG.md and an ADR. Design rule (decided here, applies to #355-#365): formats and validations are designed together in one field descriptor and one test suite, but kept as separate layers of one pipeline: raw input -> parse by input format -> canonical value -> normalise (#364) -> validate the canonical value (#355/#356) -> store canonical -> format for display. Validation never runs on a display string, and a display format never changes what is stored. | 1 | 104 | Licensing & Protection | Full image restored on different hardware | Active install with a valid licence on machine A; machine B with different hardware | 1) Take a full disk image of machine A. 2) Restore and boot it on B. 3) Open the app. Expected: 'activation required' state, no domain module opens, a suspicious-event record is logged. | Risk if it fails: If it opens, copy protection is worthless and on-prem sales are unprotected. | Needs QA |
| 2 | 100 | Licensing & Protection | Full request-to-licence cycle | Fresh install with no licence | 1) Open the licence page and download the .pemsreq. 2) Issue a term licence with the vendor tool. 3) Upload it. Expected: activates with the right edition/modules. 4) Upload the same licence on a second install: it must be rejected. | Risk if it fails: A transferable licence means lost revenue. | Needs QA |
| 3 | 105 | Licensing & Protection | Setting the clock back | A 30-day licence with 20 days consumed | Set the system clock back 20 days and start the app. Expected: soft lock/clock-tamper warning; consumed days are not restored. | Risk if it fails: Unlimited free renewal. | Needs QA |
| 4 | 102 | Licensing & Protection | Licence type boundaries | A term licence expiring tonight; a lifetime licence; a trial licence | 1) Let the term licence reach its end date: the app drops to restricted mode. 2) Check expiry at Jalali midnight. 3) A lifetime licence shows no expiry warning. 4) Renew a term licence before and after expiry. | Risk if it fails: An off-by-one error locks a legitimate customer. | Needs QA |
| 5 | 116 | Licensing & Protection | Hardware replacement | Active install | Request a re-host, vendor approves, new licence; the old licence is disabled on the old machine. Exceeding the cap requires manual approval. | Risk if it fails: A legitimate customer stays down after a server failure. | Needs QA |
| 6 | 110 | Licensing & Protection | No bypass present | Final release package | Scan the package for bypass flags; boot without a licence in dev/staging/prod: all three lock. The allowlisted surfaces stay open. | Risk if it fails: Selling an unlocked package. | Needs QA |
| 7 | 106 | Licensing & Protection | Issuing tool only at the vendor | Customer package | Open the customer package: no issuing tool and no private key exist. | Risk if it fails: Exposure of licence issuing. | Needs QA |
| 8 | 10 | Metadata Core & Dynamic Platform | Create an entity purely with metadata | Clean instance | Create a 'lab sample' entity with 10 fields in the UI, publish, create/edit/delete a record with no deploy. Expected: list, form, search, export and audit work. | Risk if it fails: The core of dynamism does not work. | Needs QA |
| 9 | 13 | Metadata Core & Dynamic Platform | Field type change with data | Entity with 100k records | Change a field from text to number: risk preview, two-step approval, backup; then roll back. Expected: data returns intact. | Risk if it fails: Loss of customer data. | Needs QA |
| 10 | 12 | Metadata Core & Dynamic Platform | Server-side paging/filter | 500k records | Filter and sort the list; response under 500 ms. The total count respects RLS. | Risk if it fails: Repeat of a known paging regression. | Needs QA |
| 11 | 178 | Access, RLS, Sessions & Security | Data leakage | Two users with different scope | Read user A's record as user B through list, search, export, report, API and AI: it must never appear. | Risk if it fails: Leak of customer confidential information. | Needs QA |
| 12 | 44 | Dynamic Form Builder | Build an inspection form | Builder role | Build a 20-field form with 2 tabs, a child table and conditions; preview as different roles; publish. Create a second version: old records stay intact. | Risk if it fails: A failed form builder means a failed product. | Needs QA |
| 13 | 67 | Workflow Studio & BPMS | Build a workflow | Builder role | Build a 5-stage approval flow with parallel branches and SLA; the simulator reports no errors; publish; run an instance; let a deadline pass: escalation is recorded. | Risk if it fails: A fragile production workflow. | Needs QA |
| 14 | 69 | Workflow Studio & BPMS | Publish a new process version | 5 in-flight instances | Publish a new version: active instances continue or migrate per policy, with no errors. | Risk if it fails: Breaking in-flight processes. | Needs QA |
| 15 | 87 | Static-to-Dynamic Module Migration | Wave 1 parity gate | Old and new modules of wave 1 | Run the same scenarios on old and new: output and permissions are identical; then the static version is removed. | Risk if it fails: Regression after migration. | Needs QA |
| 16 | 170 | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Signed update and rollback | Install of version N | Apply package N+1; a tampered package is rejected; roll back to N. | Risk if it fails: A bad update locks the customer. | Needs QA |
| 17 | 167 | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Fresh install | Clean Windows Server | Run the installer, validator, first-run wizard and admin login in under 30 minutes. | Risk if it fails: Customers will not install without support. | Needs QA |
| 18 | 141 | Online Demo | Demo reset | Demo with visitor changes | Run the nightly reset: data exactly matches the sample pack; the demo licence expires. | Risk if it fails: A broken demo loses sales. | Needs QA |
| 19 | 206 | Persian Localization, RTL & Jalali Calendar | Jalali calendar | Persian user | Dates in list/form/report are Jalali; stored Gregorian; range filter correct; leap years 1403 and 1407 correct. | Risk if it fails: Wrong dates in reports. | Needs QA |
| 20 | 243 | Existing PEMS Capabilities (estimated from code) | Existing workflow runtime | Existing sample | Run the document-approval workflow (parallel and sequential) and check the history. | Risk if it fails: Everything else relies on the runtime without QA. | Needs QA |
| 21 | 242 | Existing PEMS Capabilities (estimated from code) | Authorization and SoD | Sample roles | Trigger SoD, enter an out-of-scope record, check AuthorizationAuditLog. | Risk if it fails: Foundation of RLS. | Needs QA |
| 22 | 240 | Existing PEMS Capabilities (estimated from code) | Authentication and FIDO | User with a security key | Login, lockout after 5 attempts, step-up. | Risk if it fails: Baseline security. | Needs QA |

Expected: database created if allowed, all migrations applied, health = 200 UpToDate, one FirstRun/Startup row in the history, seeders succeeded. | Risk if it fails: New customers and the demo cannot start without a DBA. | Needs QA |
| 24 | 309 | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Update to a release with new migrations | A database installed by the previous release; the new release adds at least one migration. | 1) Deploy the new build. 2) Start it. Expected: only the new migrations are applied, history shows one Update row, data is unchanged. Repeat with Database__MigrateOnStartup=false: the page shows UpdateAvailable and Apply performs the update. | Risk if it fails: Updates need manual SQL or lose data. | Needs QA |
| 25 | 310 | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Schema without migration history is left alone | A database with tables but no __EFMigrationsHistory (restored from an old script). | Start the app. Expected: warning in the log, the page shows Unmanaged schema, Apply is not offered, no table is changed. | Risk if it fails: A restored database is corrupted by a second install. | Needs QA |
| 26 | 310 | Installation, Deployment & Updates | Database Update page access and apply | Users with and without the SystemAdmin role. | 1) Anonymous and a normal user cannot open /Admin/DatabaseUpdate. 2) SystemAdmin sees state, pending list and history. 3) Apply without the backup checkbox is refused; with it, applies. | Risk if it fails: Anyone could change the schema, or admins cannot update. | Needs QA |

## Sheet: Roadmap
| Epic / Milestone | When | Priority | Areas Involved | Rationale | Tasks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M0 - Baseline and foundation decisions | Now | P0 | FND, LIC, BRD, DEM, WEB, MIG, CMP, QA | Without the decisions on storage, UI technology, libraries, the licensing threat model and editions nothing later is stable. Decisions are cheap and unblock everything. In parallel run a real build/test and clean secrets from the repo. |  |
| M1 - Dynamic core, components and licensing (Alpha) | Now | P0 | DYN, CMP, LIC, SEC, LOC | These block everything else: metadata registry, dynamic API with RLS, safe schema change, grid/form, Persian/Jalali, and the whole licence chain (fingerprint, install seal, request file, signed licence, vendor tool, anti-clone). Selling without a licence and without dynamism is meaningless. |  |
| M2 - Builders, demo and install (Beta) | Next | P0 | FRM, MOD, WFL, MIG, DEM, INS, BRD | The form, module and workflow builders plus the first migration wave (wave 1) prove the engine works. The demo and the installer with signed updates allow sales to begin. |  |
| M3 - Commercial release 1.0: demo, website, brand and legal | Next | P1 | LIC, BRD, DEM, WEB, INS, DEV, CVM, HST | Sellable packaging: licence activation portal, website, sales material, pilot customer, licence upgrade/transfer/renewal and support, plus the customer registry and release process needed before the first customer is live. |  |
| M4 - Platform completion and module migration | Next | P1 | DYN, CMP, FRM, MOD, WFL, MIG, SEC, INT, QA, LOC, RPT, AI, OPS, EDM, EAM | Release 1.0 must be able to host every wave: the rest of the platform P1 work, wave 2, mobile, SSO, security and the report builder. |  |
| M5 - Competitive features (P2) | Later | P2 | All | Capabilities that make selling easier but do not block 1.0. |  |
| M6 - Later (P3) | Later | P3 | All | Highest uncertainty and lowest sales impact. |  |
| Suggested order for the first 60 days (after owner approval) |  |  |  |  |  |
| Week 1: F_BASE (real build/test), F_SECRET (repo secrets), F_ADR, and the F_STORE / F_UI / F_LIBS decisions with small prototypes. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Weeks 1-2: owner decisions: VM/container policy (L_TIMES), edition matrix (L_EDITION), pricing (B_PRICE), legal structure (B_ENTITY). |  |  |  |  |  |
| Weeks 2-4: L_MODEL (threat model) and L_FP + L_INSTALLID (proof: an image on other hardware does not run); C_AUDIT; M_AUDIT. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Weeks 3-8: vertical slice: D_OBJ + D_API + C_GRID + FRM_RUN on one sample entity (migration wave 1 pilot); S_RLS in parallel. |  |  |  |  |  |
| Weeks 5-8: L_REQ -> L_LICFMT -> L_VENDOR -> L_UI -> L_FIRSTRUN (the complete licence chain) and LOC_DEFAULT / LOC_CAL. |  |  |  |  |  |
| End of 60 days: an internal demo of 'build a module in the UI and run it' with the machine-locked licence working; then M2. |  |  |  |  |  |

## Sheet: Licensing Design
| PEMS licensing design - machine and install-time locked (on-prem, offline) |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Owner need: even if a whole machine is imaged and deployed elsewhere it must not work; a file is generated on the customer server, returned to us, and we deliver the real licence; lifetime or timed. Limits: no offline scheme is unbreakable - the goal is to defeat casual copying and add contractual backing. |  |  |  |  |
| Step | Stage | Description | Backlog rows |  |
| 1 | Install | The installer runs; first start creates a random Install ID and a key pair; the private key is made non-exportable with DPAPI/TPM; install time and a monotonic counter are written to the 'install seal' (DB + an independent file). | #99 |  |
| 2 | Request | Without a licence the system starts in 'activation required' mode and creates a .pemsreq file: hashed fingerprint, Install ID, install time, version, customer id, nonce, install-key signature. | #98 #100 #108 |  |
| 3 | Return to vendor | The customer gives the file to us through the website portal (WEB_ACT) or by hand; the product itself never calls out. | #161 #123 |  |
| 4 | Issue | The vendor tool (L_VENDOR) validates the signature and structure, selects edition/modules/limits/type (lifetime or term) and signs a .pemslic with the offline private key; the licence is bound to the nonce, Install ID and fingerprint hash of that request. | #101 #102 #106 #107 |  |
| 5 | Activate | The customer uploads the licence (or a zip); verification: signature, Install ID match, fingerprint, seal, monotonic counter, expiry; then an automatic restart. | #103 #108 |  |
| 6 | Operate | Multiple checks at startup/periodic/sensitive operations; expiry warnings for term licences; failure means restricted read-only mode, not a crash. | #103 #105 #115 |  |
| 7 | Renew/transfer | Renewal with a small file; hardware replacement with a new request and revocation of the old licence (re-host) with a cap on count. | #115 #116 #117 |  |
| Threat and defense table |  |  |  |  |
| # | Attack scenario | Defense | Residual risk | Rows |
| 1 | Full machine image on different hardware | Multi-signal fingerprint plus a non-exportable install key (DPAPI/TPM); the seal cannot be opened | Low | #98 #99 |
| 2 | VM clone (same virtual hardware appearance) | VM/container policy (L_TIMES); virtual TPM / hypervisor identifiers; monotonic counter and lifecycle trail | Medium - needs a commercial decision and contract | #97 #104 #120 |
| 3 | Restoring an old snapshot on the same machine | Monotonic counter plus audit hash chain; a lower counter is suspicious; re-activation required | Medium | #104 |
| 4 | Setting the clock back for a term licence | Sealed last-seen time; a jump back causes a soft lock | Low | #105 |
| 5 | Binary patch / removing the licence check | Several independent checks, obfuscation, assembly signing, integrity checks | Medium (no software defense is absolute) | #103 #118 |
| 6 | Copying or sharing a licence file between customers | The licence is bound to Install ID + fingerprint + nonce; another install rejects it | Low | #101 |
| 7 | Leak of the private signing key | Offline key/HSM, two-person control, public-key rotation via update | Critical if it happens | #107 |
| 8 | Licence issuing tool inside the customer package | Two flags (build-time/run-time); package allowlist scan | Low | #106 #119 |
| 9 | Leftover bypass from development | No bypass flag; a signed dev licence; release test | Low | #110 |
| 10 | Recovery after hardware failure (legitimate user, new machine) | Official re-host path with vendor approval and a cap on count | Operational | #116 #171 |

## Sheet: Lessons
| Project memory - facts and lessons |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| - | PEMS is a brand-new project. Its task source of truth is this workbook (plan/PEMS_Backlog_Roadmap.xlsx). |  |  |  |
| - | Repository: PEMS-cmms-edit-empoleeys-master (public GitHub under a personal account - ownership/licence to be clarified, row B_ENTITY). Stack: .NET 10, EF Core 10, SQL Server, Hangfire, QuestPDF, Fido2, Serilog, Ollama. |  |  |  |
| - | Working language of the product is Persian. The app's default culture is currently en and the calendar is deliberately Gregorian (Program.cs) - rows LOC_DEFAULT and LOC_CAL. |  |  |  |
| - | Licensing: on-prem, machine + install-time lock; request/licence file process; lifetime or term (a time period); product name is PEMS (decided). |  |  |  |
| - | Old audit workbooks are kept in '0 - trash'; the previous Persian edition of this workbook is kept there too. |  |  |  |
| - | Not yet done as of 2026-10-15: build/test run, licensing, installer, builders. |  |  |  |
| # | Lesson | Reference | Explanation | PEMS rows |
| 1 | Registry-driven, not code-driven | Titan Overview | Apps/models/fields/statuses/licences are database rows and one generic CRUD/export/import engine runs them; adding an app means seeding metadata. | #10 #12 |
| 2 | A registry change must not require a rebuild | Titan #61 | A static export forced a full rebuild and redeploy after each registry change; PEMS is server-rendered but must keep this property. | #28 |
| 3 | Registry cache + ETag + gzip | Titan #50 | Database slowness was fixed by caching the metadata registry and conditional GET; build it in from the start. | #28 |
| 4 | One contract for server-side paging | Titan #49 #136 | It stayed incomplete twice; use one query contract and one grid, not patch by patch. | #12 #32 |
| 5 | Changing a field type on live data is the riskiest operation | Titan #17 | Needs guided ALTER, rollback and a data-risk warning. | #13 |
| 6 | Licence was enforced only in production | Titan #143 #204 | Staging/local booted without a licence, and a LICENSE_BYPASS flag was left behind. | #110 |
| 7 | The first-run licence flow was fragile | Titan #142 #144 | The setup token was not created in one case; the rate limiter was tied to the DB and requests hung. | #109 |
| 8 | Offer the fingerprint in the UI, not the terminal | Titan #141 | Copy/download of the fingerprint from the page; delivery as zip and automatic restart. | #108 #100 |
| 9 | Licence issuing in a separate tool, never in the customer package | Titan #145 | Two flags (build/run); the superowner tool stays with the vendor. | #106 #119 |
| 10 | Public surfaces must not need a licence | Titan #163 #168 | An explicit allowlist; app licensing derived from model licences. | #111 |
| 11 | Signing keys are a permanent operational responsibility | Titan #207 | A key procedure is needed, not just code. | #107 |
| 12 | Package allowlist for releases | Titan #68 #137 | Secrets and development files remained in the package; allowlist scan. | #119 #9 |
| 13 | Signed offline updates | Titan #249 #130 #206 | A signed package, an Update page and manual support operations. | #170 #175 |
| 14 | Installer, updater and migrator are three separate jobs | Titan Instructions 13 | Fresh install strict, update tolerant, the migrator the low-level engine; never edit an applied migration. | #167 #170 |
| 15 | SaaS is in tension with the offline model | Titan #78 | Multi-tenancy was cancelled; solve the demo with an isolated instance. | #138 |
| 16 | Live brand profile from the database | Titan #98 #241 #236 | Logo/name/colors on the login page come from the DB; bugs with a broken logo and the wrong profile. | #129 |
| 17 | A mobile view must really work on every screen | Titan #177 | Responsive alone is not enough; a QA matrix. | #39 #223 |
| 18 | Library-first, even retroactively | Titan Instructions 2 | Bytecode protection, security middleware, password hashing, TOTP, server-side Excel, migrator, logging, virtualized lists... | #6 #187 |
| 19 | No automated tests is expensive | Titan #20 #117 | PEMS has 1,403 tests but the dynamic engine needs its own independent suite. | #203 #1 |
| 20 | The owner may commit in another terminal | Titan Instructions 10 | Re-run git status before commit and stage only specific paths. | - |
| 21 | Editable permission matrix on unit/user forms | Titan #3 | A permission UI instead of fixed seeds. | #60 |
| 22 | Jalali + Gregorian calendar as a default view | Titan #179 | Essential for the Iranian domain. | #206 #33 |
| 23 | Offline-only assistant | Titan #181 #182 | The hosting decision for an offline LLM was already made. | #216 |
| 24 | Code-first means the app applies the schema itself | PEMS (2026-10-21) | PEMS had EF migrations but Program.cs only ran seeders, so every new environment depended on a hand-run SQL script. Now the app tells an install (empty) from an update (pending) and from an unmanaged schema (never touched). Create-database must run before anything that connects (Hangfire installs its schema on configuration), migrations run before seeders, non-model DDL (full-text) is also a migration, and a CI drift gate keeps model and migrations in step. Keep an off-switch for reviewed-script environments. | #309 #310 #311 |
| 25 | Give pipelines a details-free health endpoint for the schema | PEMS (2026-10-22) | A deploy that uploads files cannot see whether the first start installed the database. An anonymous /health/database (200 only when UpToDate, state name otherwise) lets the pipeline wait for the install/update and fail the run, without exposing connection strings or versions. Raise the IIS startupTimeLimit because the first run builds the whole schema. | #310 #312 |
| 26 | One settings contract: files for settings, files for secrets, nothing in code | PEMS (2026-10-23) | Settings were spread over appsettings.*.json, in-code '?? default' values and a built-in administrator password in a public repository. Keep one key set per environment in committed env files, secrets only in git-ignored files or environment variables, required keys that fail fast, and tests that keep key sets identical and scan the committed files for secrets. A dependency-free reader is needed where the configuration packages cannot be referenced (design-time EF factory). | #315 #316 #317 |
| 27 | Study the whole project before building a library | PEMS (2026-10-23) | A source-level audit gave hard numbers (2,587 hand-written inputs vs 202 bound fields, 342 tables, 632 inline styles, 4 offline violations) that sized the waves and exposed a rule breach. Keep the audit script and rerun it per wave; use its counts as a CI ratchet. | #29 #327 |
| 28 | Make the component contract technology-neutral | PEMS (2026-10-23) | Dynamic forms need metadata-driven rendering while 300 existing pages need MVC compatibility. Describing fields/columns/views as descriptors keeps the rendering technology (Razor components or View Components) replaceable. | #319 #321 |
| 29 | One owner per field between the plan file and the issue tracker | PEMS (2026-10-23) | Two systems drift unless each field has a single owner and a script compares them. Plan ID and issue key link the records; the tracker owns status, the workbook owns scope; changes to the workbook go through pull requests. | #335 #336 |
## Sheet: Supportability Matrix
| Supportability Matrix - what PEMS supports, and up to which limits |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Created 2026-10-18 by owner instruction. Update it in every finished task (Instructions rule). Builder limits and all supported values come from here. Status: Planned, Decided, Supported, Supported (unverified), TBD (to be measured), Deprecated, Not supported. |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| ID | Category | Capability / limit | Component | Supported value / limit | Applies from version | Status | Verified by (backlog rows) | Notes |
| SM-01 | Deployment | Physical Windows Server | Core / installer | Supported platform; exact Windows Server versions to be fixed by the environment validator. | - | Planned | #167 #168 #98 |  |
| SM-02 | Deployment | Virtual machines (VMware, Hyper-V, Proxmox) | Core | Allowed. Licence uses a virtual TPM or hypervisor identifier where available plus the restore-detection counter; EULA forbids cloning. | - | Decided | #97 #104 #120 | Decision 2026-10-18. |
| SM-03 | Deployment | Docker containers | Core | Allowed with the same extra protection; production hardening pending. | - | Decided | #172 #104 | Decision 2026-10-18. |
| SM-04 | Deployment | Offline operation (no internet) | Core | Required: no CDN, no outbound calls from the product. | - | Planned | #42 |  |
| SM-05 | Database | SQL Server versions | Core | TBD - fixed by the first real build/test run and the environment validator. | - | TBD | #1 #168 |  |
| SM-06 | Database | Upgrade path | Core | Upgrade from any supported release; target is N-2. | - | Planned | #262 #274 |  |
| SM-07 | Licence | Licence types | Core | Lifetime and term (a time period), plus trial and demo. No count-based licences. | - | Decided | #102 | Decision 2026-10-15. |
| SM-08 | Licence | User limit | Core | No licence limit on users. | - | Decided | - | Decision 2026-10-18; user metering cancelled. |
| SM-09 | Licence | Maintenance | Core | Perpetual licence plus annual maintenance; maintenance controls which updates can be installed. | - | Planned | #275 #130 | Decision 2026-10-18. |
| SM-10 | Versioning | Product version | Core | The installed product version is the core version. | - | Planned | #269 #282 | Decision 2026-10-18. |
| SM-11 | Versioning | Module versions | Module | Each module has its own version and a declared compatible core range. | - | Planned | #282 #283 #284 | Decision 2026-10-18. |
| SM-12 | Builders | Custom modules per instance | Core | TBD - set from measured limits. | - | TBD | #281 #66 |  |
| SM-13 | Builders | Forms per module | Core | TBD - set from measured limits. | - | TBD | #281 #66 |  |
| SM-14 | Builders | Fields per entity | Core | TBD - set from measured limits. | - | TBD | #281 #11 |  |

| SM-16 | Builders | Child-table rows per form | Core | TBD; target 200 rows without slowness. | - | TBD | #281 #48 |  |
| SM-17 | Data volume | Records per entity with filter and sort under 500 ms | Core | Target 500,000 records. | - | Planned | #12 #202 |  |
| SM-18 | Concurrency | Concurrent users tested | Core | TBD from the performance suite; not limited by the licence. | - | TBD | #202 |  |
| SM-19 | Localization | Persian RTL default, English optional | Core | Planned; current default culture is English. | - | Planned | #205 |  |
| SM-20 | Localization | Calendars | Core | Jalali and Gregorian; stored as Gregorian/UTC. | - | Planned | #206 |  |
| SM-21 | Clients | Supported browsers | Core | TBD - set with the responsive QA matrix. | - | TBD | #39 |  |
| SM-22 | Clients | Offline mobile work execution | Module | Planned; not yet supported. | - | Planned | #223 |  |
| SM-23 | Security | Authentication | Core | ASP.NET Identity with lockout and WebAuthn/FIDO2 step-up (estimated from code). | - | Supported (unverified) | #240 #241 | Acceptance tests not run. |
| SM-24 | Domain | Existing modules: EDMS/VDR, CMMS, permit to work, inventory, production | Modules | Present as static modules (estimated from code). | - | Supported (unverified) | #250 #244 | Acceptance tests not run. |
| SM-25 | AI | Local AI (Ollama, OCR, semantic search) | Module | Present (estimated from code). | - | Supported (unverified) | #248 | Acceptance tests not run. |
| SM-26 | Deployment | Database install (first run) and update (later runs) | Core / startup | Fully code-first on SQL Server: the database is created if missing, an empty database is installed and an existing one is updated by EF Core migrations at startup or from Admin > Database Update; full-text search is a migration (install the SQL Server Full-Text feature BEFORE the first run). Switch off the automatic run with Database:MigrateOnStartup=false. | - | Supported (unverified) | #309 #310 #167 #262 | Verified on SQL Server LocalDB (install, update, unmanaged schema, full app start). Not yet verified on the demo host or on SQL Server with Full-Text. |
| SM-27 | Deployment | Schema state inspection | Core / Admin | Admin > System Monitoring > Database Update (SystemAdmin) and anonymous GET /health/database (200 only when UpToDate). | - | Supported (unverified) | #310 #312 | Page access control checked (anonymous redirected to login); not yet viewed as admin in a browser. |
| SM-28 | Deployment | Database restored without migration history | Core | Reported as 'Unmanaged schema' and never modified automatically; a baseline tool is planned. | - | Planned | #310 #313 | Detection is tested; adoption is not supported yet. |
| SM-29 | Branding | Release notes on the login page | Core / login | Planned: bilingual, offline release notes of the installed release shown to anonymous visitors. | - | Planned | #314 | Not started. |
| SM-30 | Deployment | Configuration and secrets | Core | Settings in env/<name>.env and secrets in env/<name>.secrets.env for local, docker, demo, uat and production-kangan; PEMS_ENV selects the environment; real environment variables override; required settings fail fast; appsettings.json is empty. | - | Supported (unverified) | #315 #316 | Verified on a developer machine (LocalDB). Demo pipeline, Docker and the Linux VM kit not yet exercised. |
| SM-31 | UI | Reusable UI components (PEMS.UI) | PEMS.UI | Planned: every field, table, badge, skeleton, modal and page template is a component rendered from descriptors; RTL/LTR, offline, tokens only. | - | Planned | #319-#334 | Audit baseline recorded 2026-10-23; no component exists yet. |
| SM-32 | Delivery | Plan workbook <-> the issue tracker consistency | tools/plan-sync | Export of the backlog to tracker CSV and an offline consistency check; push/pull planned. | - | Planned | #335 #336 #337 | Export and check verified on the workbook; the issue tracker side not set up. |
| SM-33 | Delivery | Schema migrations deployed separately from the data layer | PEMS.Migrations / PEMS.Persistence | Migrations, snapshot and design-time factory live in PEMS.Migrations; a data-layer-only change leaves PEMS.Migrations.dll byte-identical (build with DebugType=none), so the difference upload skips it | - | Done | #354 #351 | Add a migration with the --project PEMS.Migrations option. Verified 2026-10-25: Persistence 29 MB -> 6 MB, Migrations 23 MB. |
| SM-34 | Deployment | Pre-production staging environment | Core / deploy pipeline | staging branch -> staging subdomain, own database, env/staging.env, STAGING_* secrets; same reusable pipeline as demo | - | Done | #351 | Verified 2026-10-25 (run 40208748572). |
| SM-35 | Delivery | Deployments upload only changed files and skip repeated tests | Deploy pipeline | FTP difference upload (sync state file); tag gate-pass/<source tree hash> skips build, drift gate and tests for an identical tree; first full upload ~245 MB, later ~34 MB | - | Done | #351 #354 | Package slimmed 285 -> 245 MB. |
| SM-36 | Security | No built-in credentials in source, container or tool files | Core / compose / tools | No default or hard-coded password: the first administrator comes only from Seed__Admin__Password; users created from an employee get a random one-time password; docker-compose publishes ports on 127.0.0.1 only and requires SQL_SA_PASSWORD | - | Supported (unverified) | #9 #317 | Implemented 2026-10-26 (SCRUM-17); status becomes Done when the build and EnvironmentFilesTests and TemporaryPasswordGeneratorTests have run green. Environments created before 2026-10-23 need rotation (#317); the old values stay in git history (#374, on hold). |
| SM-37 | Security | Production password policy | Core / env files | Production installations: minimum length 12, digit required, no upper-case/symbol rule, lockout 5 attempts / 10 minutes; other environments keep length 8 | - | Supported (unverified) | #318 #9 | Decision 2026-10-26 (NIST SP 800-63B, OWASP ASVS V2). HSTS age, cookie sliding expiry and the migration command timeout are settings. Unverified until EnvironmentFilesTests have run. |
| SM-38 | Security | Administrator account: last-admin guard and second-admin reset | Core / Identity (UsersController, AdminAccountService) | The last active SystemAdmin cannot be deactivated or removed from the role (checked on the server). Another administrator can reset an administrator account: random temporary password shown once and valid for 24 hours, forced password change, lockout cleared, passkeys and authenticator key removed, all sessions revoked. Resetting your own account is refused. | - | Done | SCRUM-354 | MFA is only flagged (MfaEnrollmentRequired) until login MFA exists (SCRUM-188 / SCRUM-206). The user edit form can still change an admin password directly (audited as AdminConsole). |
| SM-39 | Security | Security events for administrator recovery | Core / Identity (RecoveryEventService, SecurityEvents) | Every administrator recovery attempt (success or failure) is stored in an append-only table with actor, target, method, IP and UTC time, notifies all active administrators and revokes the target sessions. Read-only list at Admin > Security Events. Failure reasons are fixed codes, never free text. | - | Done | SCRUM-357 | Forwarding to a SIEM is SCRUM-212 (the table has a ForwardedToSiemAt marker). All three recovery paths (SCRUM-354, SCRUM-355, SCRUM-356) use it. |
| SM-40 | Security | Recovery codes and server-side break-glass for administrators | Core / Identity (AdminRecoveryCodeService, BreakGlassService, AccountRecovery, RecoveryCodes, BreakGlass) | Recovery codes: 10 one-time codes (80-bit, PBKDF2 hash + salt only), shown once with copy/print/download, regenerated from the user menu (current password required; old codes revoked). /AccountRecovery: user name + code + new password; wrong codes count toward Identity lockout; 5 POSTs per IP per 15 min (Security:Recovery:RateLimit). Break-glass: file pems-recovery.request in Security:BreakGlass:RequestFolder opens /break-glass for 15 minutes, only from localhost or Security:BreakGlass:ManagementNetworks (also checked against X-Forwarded-For/Forwarded), otherwise 404; single use (file deleted). Vendor-signed token (ECDSA P-256, max 24 h, machine fingerprint) off by default. | - | Done | SCRUM-355 SCRUM-356 | The first-run wizard (SCRUM-188) will call the same code service. MFA re-enrolment is a flag until login MFA exists (SCRUM-188/206). The machine fingerprint for vendor tokens comes from settings until the licence engine provides it. Behind a reverse proxy the per-IP limit counts the proxy address. |
| SM-41 | UI | Searchable multi-select field | Web UI (pems-multiselect.js, vendored Tom Select 2.6.2) | Any <select multiple data-pems-multiselect> becomes a searchable multi-select with removable chips and option groups; offline (wwwroot/lib/tom-select, Apache-2.0); RTL/LTR and the PEMS themes; works without JavaScript as a native multi-select. First used for user roles in Admin > Users. | - | Done | SCRUM-407 | Client-side search over the options in the page; large or server-side lists (paging, RLS) are SCRUM-57. The full component API comes with SCRUM-352. |
| SM-42 | Deployment | Database upgrade from every supported release, pre-update backup and rollback | Core / Persistence (DatabaseUpdateService), deploy/db, Migration Chain Gate workflow | A database of every release listed in deploy/db/supported-releases.json (now 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-25) upgrades to the current build; CI proves it with representative data in every table it can fill and compares the result with a fresh install (schema) and the row counts. Migrations shipped in a supported release are frozen by checksum. With Database__BackupBeforeUpdate=true (production, uat) every update first writes a COPY_ONLY backup (default backup folder or Database__BackupDirectory) and is not started if that fails; restoring that backup is the documented rollback. | - | Done | SCRUM-280 | No automatic deletion of old backups. Shared hosting without BACKUP permission keeps the setting off and relies on the host backup. The data check uses one generic row per table, not a customer copy; tables refused by check constraints are reported, not filled. Releases must be added to the manifest when they ship (no version numbering yet, F_CI). |
| SM-43 | Workflow | Editable module workflows guarded by module contracts | Workflow platform (WorkflowVersionValidator, PemsWorkflowContracts, WorkflowBindingBackfillSeeder), Admin > Workflow Management | All 13 module workflows (engineering document, VDR, contract, MOC, TQ, permit, NCR, IRN, maintenance strategy, part catalogue, work order lifecycle and review, work request) have an explicit binding and are editable: participants, SLA, approval strategy, conditions, labels, extra stages and transitions. Each module contract (actions the code runs, stages it reads by name, dynamic-participant hand-overs, BusinessStatus) is enforced on Validate/Publish. Running instances keep their version; the binding is moved explicitly. | - | Done | SCRUM-103 | Stage names in a contract cannot be renamed (labels can). Running instances are not migrated to a new version (SCRUM-90). Document, MOC and TQ status still come from stage names; switching them to BusinessStatus would free those names. |
| SM-44 | UI | List page component (frame, toolbar, filters, KPIs on demand, chips, grid) | PEMS.UI (pems-list-* tag helpers, pems-list.css/js) | One component for record list pages: full-window table with sticky header and bottom pager, one icon toolbar (search over the title with the shared autocomplete, filters dropdown with count badge, KPI row on demand remembered per browser, Excel, reset columns, New), removable filter chips, empty state with clear-all. Each page keeps its own columns, filters, actions and permissions. In use: Engineering Documents, VDR (wave 0). | - | Partial | SCRUM-337 | Waves W1-W4 (document control, CMMS, warehouse/procurement, admin) still to move. Saved views, column picker, pinning and virtualization are SCRUM-54. |
